+++

Spike S5 (system design, section 12) asks what one coursework instance serves: every row of
section 4.7's table, with replayed agent sessions of both shapes, hands-on and several agents a
student, at 1, 4, 8 and 16 streams and at the class's size, on the 8-bit and the 4-bit build; the
cache size the engine reports at start; and what each lever of section 4.7 changes. Every number
of section 4.7 for the coursework pool is arithmetic until then. This record replaces that table,
and the dry-run class of phase 3 is judged against it (section 14).

If the class-size runs miss a floor, the answer is the levers of section 4.7 in order, then fewer
streams and more instances per session, or the upgrade pool.

## The job

The job `s5_capacity` runs [`spikes/s5_capacity.py`](../../spikes/s5_capacity.py), whose
description says how it measures and what it is given. In short: for each shape, one run at each
number of streams it is given, then one at the class's size, each replaying synthetic agent
sessions of section 4.7's sizes through the gateway as canary students for the duration it is
given, in a session the owner has booked. Each request is sent as Claude Code sends it: adaptive thinking at its
default effort, `high`, which the pool's `reasoning_effort` mapping rewrites, and the same opening
of 20,000 tokens, Claude Code's instructions and tools, at the start of every session, followed by
the session's own text. It reads what the students got from the load generator's records, and
what each instance's engine measured from the workspace. It never changes the pool.

It posts one run of S5, `measure/S5/<build>-<lever>-<instances>/<part>`, with every number of all
its runs: one number for each quantity and condition, named `<quantity>_<shape>_<streams>`, or
`<quantity>_<shape>_class` at the class's size, the shape `hands_on` or `several_agents`
(`ttft_p95_several_agents_class`); `class_size`, the class's size the job was given; and
`numbers_posted`, how many numbers the run posted, this one included, which tells the record when
every part of a posting is there:

| Quantity | Unit | What it is |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `requests`, `failed_requests` | `count` | The requests sent, and those that failed: every request not answered 200, or whose answer or stream named one of the gateway's causes, a stream answered 200 and then ended by the gateway's error event among them |
| `failed_share` | `percent` | The failed requests in percent of the requests sent: the number the verdict reads |
| `failed_<cause>`, one for each of the gateway's causes (`failed_engine_error`, `failed_capacity_unavailable`) | `count` | The failed requests that named that cause, in their answer or in an error event of their stream. Each cause has one status, so these are counts by status and cause |
| `failed_in_stream` | `count` | The failed requests answered 200 whose stream then carried the gateway's error event; they are also counted under their cause |
| `failed_without_cause` | `count` | The failed requests that named no cause of the gateway's, such as an answer of the edge |
| `instances` | `count` | The instances that served the run |
| `ttft_median`, `ttft_p95` | `s` | Time to first token of later turns, as the students saw it |
| `first_turn_ttft_p95` | `s` | The same, of first turns |
| `stream_median`, `stream_p10` | `tokens/s` | A stream's output speed: the median, and the slowest tenth |
| `output_throughput_per_instance` | `tokens/s` | Output tokens an instance's engine produced a second, the mean over the instances |
| `prefill_speed` | `tokens/s` | Tokens the engine computed in prefill, over prefill time, the mean over the instances |
| `cached_share` | `percent` | Input tokens the engine did not compute, the mean over the instances |
| `cache_capacity` | `tokens` | The cache the engine reported at start, the smallest of the instances |
| `running_requests_peak` | `count` | The most requests an instance ran at once |
| `preemptions` | `count` | The engine's preemptions, over every instance; a preemption one instance saw is posted even when another reported nothing, and none is posted as 0 only when every instance reported it |
| `cost_per_million_output_tokens` | `USD per million output tokens` | Each instance's time in service at its configured price, over the output tokens |

A quantity with no sample behind it is not posted for that run. `failed_requests` and
`failed_share` are posted for every run that sent a request. The counts by cause,
`failed_in_stream` and `failed_without_cause` are posted only when above zero, to keep postings
short; the counts by cause and `failed_without_cause` add up to `failed_requests`.

## The verdict

A job passes when its class-size runs of both shapes each have a 95th-percentile time to first
token of later turns under 5 seconds, a median stream at 15 tokens a second or more, a slowest
tenth at 10 or more, no preemption, and a failed share of 0.5% or less. A class-size number that
misses its floor, a preemption or a failed share among them, fails the job even when another of
those numbers is missing. The floors are provisional and are read from the deployed
configuration, the keys `target_ttft_p95_seconds`, `target_stream_median_tokens_per_second` and
`target_stream_p10_tokens_per_second`, whose defaults are those values (sections 7.3 and 17); the
objectives and the inference dashboard read the same keys. The runs at a number of streams are
recorded and do not enter the verdict. Short of a miss, a job whose class-size runs lack one of
those numbers, a run with no failed share among them, is posted as not measured, as is a job whose
runs were served by two builds. The upgrade pool's spike S6 reads the same floors through the same
verdict.

The times and the speeds come from the requests that got an answer, so the verdict also reads the
failed share: without it, the survivors of a run in which most requests failed could pass. Its
floor is the key `target_failed_requests_percent`, a percentage above 0 and at most 100, which only
this verdict reads; a class-size `failed_share` above it fails the job. Sections 12 and 17
name 0.5% as that floor, the default: it is the share of failures that section 7.3's objective for
the gateway's answers allows (99.5% answered). That objective counts only the gateway's own faults,
while S5 counts every failure of a load nothing else shares.

## The rows

The record holds the whole matrix: each row is named after its quantity, and its labels say where
it was measured, so the rows of one quantity differ by their labels only. Each row is labelled with
`run`, `pool`, `instance_type`, `build`, `lever`, `instances`, `session_shape` (`hands-on` or
`several-agents`) and `streams` (the number, or `class`); the row `class_size` has all but the last
two. For example:

```toml
[[rows]]
name = "stream_median"
value = 16.5
unit = "tokens/s"
labels = { run = "8bit-baseline-1", pool = "coursework", instance_type = "g7e.2xlarge", build = "8bit", lever = "baseline", instances = "1", session_shape = "hands-on", streams = "8" }
```

The number in this example is invented.

## Running it

The 8-bit build's baseline on one instance, `8bit-baseline-1`, fails: at the class's size, the
hands-on time to first token, and the several agents' time to first token and slowest tenth, miss
their floors. The 4-bit build's, `4bit-baseline-1`, fails too: at the class's size the hands-on
shape meets every floor, and the several agents' time to first token and slowest tenth miss
theirs. During that run the pipeline's verification of its commit sent a few smoke requests to the
pool. The 4-bit build's smaller window, `4bit-smaller-window-1`, fails: at the class's size the
hands-on shape meets every floor (time to first token at the 95th percentile 2.87 s, against 3.62 s
for the baseline), and the several agents' slowest tenth now meets its floor (11.0 tokens per
second, against 9.48), while their time to first token at the 95th percentile still misses it
(37.8 s, against 42.6 s); no request failed, and no preemption or engine restart showed. One step
lower effort, `4bit-effort-one-lower-1`, fails: at the class's size the hands-on shape meets every
floor (time to first token at the 95th percentile 3.35 s), while the several agents' time to first
token at the 95th percentile (45.8 s, against 42.6 s for the baseline) and slowest tenth (7.70
tokens per second, against 9.48) miss theirs, so it does not help that shape; no request failed,
and no preemption showed. In the three 4-bit runs, every floor is met at 8 streams on one instance
and the time to first token at the 95th percentile and the slowest tenth are missed at 16, so a
class's shape that opens more than 8 streams an instance needs more instances. The class over
two instances, `4bit-baseline-2`, passes: at the class's size, four students an instance, the
hands-on shape (time to first token at the 95th percentile 2.10 s, median stream 37.6 and slowest
tenth 26.4 tokens per second) and the several agents' shape (3.09 s, 27.0 and 18.9) meet every
floor, with no failed request and no preemption. So one g7e.2xlarge on the 4-bit build carries
four students working with several agents, or eight hands-on (from `4bit-baseline-1`), and a
session books the instances that follow from the class's size (section 4.7). The cache at 16 bits
on the 4-bit build and one instance, `4bit-cache-16bit-1`, fails: its cache holds 1,034,863
tokens, half of what the 8-bit cache holds, and the several agents' shape at the class's size
misses its floors by more than the baseline does (time to first token at the 95th percentile
99.8 s, against 42.6 s for the baseline, and slowest tenth 4.0 tokens per second, against 9.48),
while the hands-on shape meets every floor (2.40 s, against 3.62 s, and 16.7 tokens per second).
At 16 streams the hands-on time to first token at the 95th percentile is 105 s. No request failed
and no preemption showed. So the lever does not make one instance carry the several agents' class,
halving the cache's capacity makes it worse, and it changes nothing in the instances a student
needs: four students an instance with several agents, as `4bit-baseline-2` shows. The step
budget, `4bit-step-budget-1` (`step_budget_tokens` 2048 instead of 8192, vLLM's per-step token
budget, on the 4-bit build and one instance), fails: the several agents' shape at the class's size
gets a better time to first token at the 95th percentile (27.4 s, against 42.6 s for the baseline)
but its slowest tenth is worse (7.1 tokens per second, against 9.48; median 17.9), so it misses the
speed floor, while the hands-on shape meets every floor (3.04 s, against 3.62 s, and 16.8 tokens
per second for the slowest tenth). A single stream runs at about 56 tokens per second. No request
failed. So the lever trades the slowest streams for the first token and changes nothing in the
instances a student needs: four students an instance with several agents, as `4bit-baseline-2`
shows. The draft head, `4bit-draft-head-1` (`draft_head_tokens` 3: the model's own multi-token
prediction head drafts 3 tokens a step, on the 4-bit build and one instance), fails: the several
agents' shape at the class's size gets a first token at the 95th percentile of 37.0 s (42.6 s for
the baseline) and a slowest tenth of 14.0 tokens per second (9.48 for the baseline; median 27.1),
so it misses the floors but is better than the baseline on both. The hands-on shape at the class's
size has a first token at the 95th percentile of 5.78 s (3.62 s for the baseline), a slowest tenth
of 18.7 tokens per second and a median of 44.7. A single stream runs at about 107 tokens per
second, about twice the baseline's 51. One request failed (an engine error) in the hands-on run of
4 streams, none elsewhere. So the lever changes nothing in the instances a student needs by
itself: four students an instance with several agents. It speeds every stream, so it is a
candidate setting for a run with more instances. The run's statuses were first posted in part (42
of 61 parts) and completed afterwards by the `entries` command of the job (issue #877); the
run is recorded from the complete set. The faster sibling, `a3b4bit-faster-sibling-1`
(the coursework pool serving nvidia/Qwen3.6-35B-A3B-NVFP4, revision 1355db6a, a mixture-of-experts
model with about 3B parameters active per token, NVIDIA's 4-bit build, instead of the 4-bit 27B
build; one instance, target concurrency 8, the engine's maximum of running sequences), fails on one
floor alone. No request failed. A single stream runs at about 215 tokens per second (the 27B 4-bit
build: about 51). The several agents' shape at the class's size gets a first token at the 95th
percentile of 11.1 s (42.6 s for the baseline; floor 5 s), a slowest tenth of 53.3 tokens per
second (9.48 for the baseline; floor 10) and a median of 60.1 (floor 15): only the first-token
floor is missed, and at 8 streams that first token is 1.17 s. The miss is queueing: at 16 streams
half of them wait for one of the engine's 8 running slots. The hands-on shape at the class's size
passes: first token at the 95th percentile of 1.8 s, a slowest tenth of 63.3 tokens per second and
a median of 88.8. By this run alone nothing changes in the instances a student needs: four
students an instance with several agents. It is by far the strongest lever measured, though: every
stream floor passes with a wide margin. Two points are open and not measured yet: whether a higher
target concurrency removes the queueing, and whether the sibling's quality on coursework tasks
holds, which is spike S3's to judge. The shared cache salt, `4bit-class-salt-1` (the pool's
`cache_salt_scope` set to `class` instead of the student, so the gateway salts the prefix cache
per class and the students share cached prefixes, on the 4-bit build and one instance; streams 1,
4, 8 and 16 of a stand-in class of 8, never a real class's size), fails: the several agents' shape
at the class's size gets a first token at the 95th percentile of 38.0 s (42.6 s for the baseline;
floor 5 s), a slowest tenth of 11.5 tokens per second (9.48 for the baseline; floor 10) and a
median of 19.1, so it misses the first-token floor alone. The hands-on shape at the class's size
passes: first token at the 95th percentile of 3.24 s (3.62 s for the baseline), a slowest tenth of
15.0 tokens per second and a median of 27.4. No request failed. So the lever changes nothing in
the instances a student needs: four students an instance with several agents, as `4bit-baseline-2`
shows. Sharing the cache across the class barely moves the several agents' numbers, which are
queueing for the engine's 8 slots. The other levers below are not run yet. Running it needs the coursework
pool deployed on its GPU (needs gpu:capacity), and is the owner's; so is the class's size, decision
5 of the design (needs human:owner-decision), with the session's instances that follow from it
(section 4.7).

The job runs in a session the owner has booked for it, and starts no machine. It is given one
number of instances, `RHI_SPIKE_INSTANCES`, and how long it lasts, `RHI_SPIKE_RUN_MINUTES`, which
has no default. Before it sends anything it reads the pool's session and the scaler's note from
the pool-state table (`RHI_SPIKE_POOL_STATE_TABLE`, the pools stack's output), and stops, as not
measured, with the reason `no-session`, `too-few-instances` or `session-too-short`, unless the
session is active, has those instances in service and lasts those minutes from now. It then
refuses, as
not measured, a run that another number served or that an instance did not serve throughout. It
also refuses, as not measured, a job whose runs were served by two instance types, since its rows
are labelled with one: hold the pool on one instance type for the whole job. The
job is started as a build of `rhi-spike-s5_capacity` on `main`, while nothing else sends to the
pool, with `RHI_SPIKE_ENDPOINT` (the gateway stack's output, a host name reached over HTTPS),
`RHI_SPIKE_WORKSPACE_URL` (the observability stack's output), `RHI_SPIKE_POOL=coursework`,
`RHI_S5_DURATION_SECONDS=<seconds>`, `RHI_SPIKE_POOL_STATE_TABLE`, `RHI_SPIKE_RUN_MINUTES=<the
job's minutes>` and the variables below. Its run label is
`<build>-<lever>-<instances>`.

For each build, `8bit` and then `4bit`:

1. **What one instance serves.** Deploy the coursework pool serving the build, with
   `max_instances = 1`, and book a session of one instance that outlasts the job. Once the
   instance is in service, start the job with
   `RHI_S5_BUILD=<build>`, `RHI_SPIKE_INSTANCES=1`, `RHI_S5_STREAMS=1,4,8,16` and
   `RHI_S5_CLASS_SIZE=<the students one instance carries in the session>`: the class's size over
   the session's instances, rounded up. Its rows labelled `instances = "1"` are what one
   instance serves, and its class-size runs judge one instance at its share of the class.
2. **The class's size over the session's instances**, when the session needs more than one
   instance. Deploy the pool with `max_instances` at the session's instances or more, and book a
   session of that many instances over the job's time (section 5.6). Once they are in service,
   start the job with `RHI_S5_BUILD=<build>`,
   `RHI_SPIKE_INSTANCES=<the session's instances>`, `RHI_S5_CLASS_SIZE=<the class's size>` and
   `RHI_S5_STREAMS=1`, the sweep being the first job's.
3. **Write the commit's runs into this record**, from the repository's root, with the instance type
   the pool ran:

   ```sh
   gh api --paginate "repos/{owner}/{repo}/commits/<sha>/statuses" |
       env PYTHONPATH=src:services/common:services/gateway:services/reporter:services/engine/lifecycle:spikes \
       uv run --locked python spikes/s5_capacity.py record --commit <sha> --instance-type <type>
   ```

   It reads every page `gh api --paginate` writes, and each run from its newest posting alone: a
   job retried under the same run label is recorded as the retry posted it, never joined to parts
   the earlier attempt left on the commit, even in the same second (the statuses' ids tell them
   apart, and a status `gh` lists twice, on two pages, is read once). A run is a pass only from one
   whole posting. The job posts `numbers_posted`, so a posting with fewer numbers listed than it
   says, in whatever part it is, is written pending, with the rows of the parts that are; written
   again once every part is listed, it is the pass. A posting that began before the
   one before it had ended (two attempts of one run label at once) cannot be told from it: the run
   is written pending with no row. Run that job again from a later commit, alone. It writes each run of S5 on the commit with
   `tools/measurements/record.py`, then names each row after its quantity and gives it its labels,
   the build, the lever and the instances read from the run label. A run posted as not measured
   becomes a pending run with no row. Add `--run <run label>`, repeated, to write only the runs
   named. A commit whose runs ran on two instance types (a larger instance on the same commit as the
   baseline) is written once for each, each time with `--run` naming that type's runs and
   `--instance-type` giving it: a run the record already holds measured on another pool or instance
   type is refused, and nothing is written.

**The levers** of section 4.7, in their order, each against the baseline run of the same build and
instances (`RHI_S5_LEVER=baseline`, the default). For each, deploy the setting, run steps 1 to 3
with `RHI_S5_LEVER=<lever>`, and deploy the baseline back:

| Lever | `RHI_S5_LEVER` | What is deployed or given |
| --- | --- | --- |
| The 4-bit build | `baseline`, with `RHI_S5_BUILD=4bit` | The pool serving the 4-bit build: the second build above |
| Reasoning effort | `effort-one-lower` | The pool's `reasoning_effort` mapping one step lower for Claude Code's default: `high = "medium"` in the place of `high = "xhigh"`. The job's requests ask for `high`, as Claude Code does, so the engine is asked for the effort the mapping gives |
| A smaller window | `smaller-window` | Nothing on the pool: the job is given `RHI_S5_WINDOW_TOKENS=100000`, and each session compacts as Claude Code does at that window, its next turn starting a new transcript, the opening then a summary of 5,000 tokens of new text, computed in full but for the opening. The baseline's sessions never reach Claude Code's own window of 200,000 tokens |
| The cache | `cache-16bit` | The pool's `kv_cache_bits = 16`, against the 8-bit cache of the baseline |
| The draft head | `draft-head` | The pool's `draft_head_tokens` |
| The step budget | `step-budget` | A smaller `step_budget_tokens` |
| A shared cache salt | `class-salt` | The pool's `cache_salt_scope = "class"`. Every session of the job opens with the same 20,000 tokens, as Claude Code's do, then its own text: with the class's salt the students share the opening's cache, and the first turns and the cached share show the gain against the baseline's per-student salt |
| A larger instance | `larger-instance` | A larger instance type of the same family in the pool |
| More instances | `baseline` | Step 2 at more instances: the run label carries them |
| The faster sibling | `faster-sibling` | The pool serving Qwen3.6-35B-A3B, with `RHI_S5_BUILD` naming its build |
